| resolved | 2026-06-25 | M0118-0009 prepared-transactions{,-cic}/stats (design 0118-0110) | LANDED enabler (NOT a promotion): same-backend two-phase commit. goopg had NO 2PC — `PREPARE TRANSACTION 's1'` did not parse (mis-lexed as the prepared-statement PREPARE with name TRANSACTION), so every permutation of prepared-transactions, prepared-transactions-cic and stats (its s1_prepare_a/commit_prepared_a/rollback_prepared_a steps) diverged at the first prepare step. New parser AST PrepareTransactionStmt/CommitPreparedStmt/RollbackPreparedStmt (parsePrepare branches on the TRANSACTION keyword; parseCommit/parseRollback branch on the unreserved PREPARED word, lexed as an identifier; gid via parseStrLit) + same-backend executor internal/server/twophase.go. Every target spec PREPAREs and COMMIT/ROLLBACK PREPAREs the gid from the SAME session that does nothing in between, so goopg keeps the prepared transaction OPEN as the connection's active txn (its writes, heavyweight locks and SSI predicate locks all persist), records connTxState.preparedGid (MarkPrepared/PreparedGid/ClearPrepared; End() clears it), and finalises COMMIT/ROLLBACK PREPARED by re-entering executeOneSimpleStmt with a synthetic CommitStmt/RollbackStmt — reusing the CANONICAL commit/rollback path verbatim (SSI pre-commit dangerous-structure check, deferred DROP INDEX/ATTACH PARTITION/inheritance/DROP TABLE, NOTIFY publish, connTx.End) so there is NO parallel commit path to drift (sibling-paths discipline) and the commit-time SSI check fires at COMMIT PREPARED exactly as upstream. PREPARE TRANSACTION outside a txn block → 25P01; PREPARE in an aborted block → 25P02; unknown gid on finalise → 42704 (same-backend only — cross-backend lookup deferred). isTwoPhaseStmt keeps the three statements out of the single-statement plan-cache pre-plan (like isNotifyStmt; planner.Plan has no node for them). Files: internal/parser/{ast.go,parser.go,twophase_test.go}, internal/server/{conn_tx.go,twophase.go,dispatch.go}, internal/testport/twophase_commit_test.go, docs/design/0118-0110 + README. Gates: TestParseTwoPhaseCommit (parser) + TestPort_TwoPhaseCommitSameBackend (e2e: commit-prepared visibility incl. cross-session isolation of the uncommitted prepared row, rollback-prepared discard, 25P01, 42704) PASS; internal/parser + internal/server units PASS; build+vet clean; pgbench smoke = pre-commit. | full promotion of prepared-transactions{,-cic} + stats | THREE residual gaps. (1) prepared-transactions-cic (probed, STATUS=defer): mechanism works end-to-end — the held prepared txn keeps its MVCC slot active so CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY correctly waits for it and unblocks at COMMIT PREPARED; first divergence advanced from "parse error at p1" all the way to the final cic2/c1 timing. ONLY residual = goopg's CIC active-slot wait (mvcc.WaitForSlotsToCommit, design 0118-0031) does NOT honour the session lock_timeout, whereas PG's WaitForLockers/VirtualXactLock does, so PG cancels cic2 with 55P03 "canceling statement due to lock timeout" (lock_timeout=10) instead of waiting. NEXT: thread lock_timeout into the CIC active-slot wait and abort with 55P03. (2) prepared-transactions (1500 perms, SERIALIZABLE SSI across prepared xacts): mechanism in place (held xact keeps its SSI predicate locks; SSI check fires at COMMIT PREPARED) but the full permutation set must be verified byte-for-byte and any first-committer/conflict-ordering gaps closed — NOT run this loop (budget). (3) stats: needs the cumulative pg_stat_* subsystem (pg_stat_get_function_calls/numscans/…, pg_stat_force_next_flush, track_functions, stats_fetch_consistency, pg_stat_slru) in addition to 2PC. Deferred 2PC scope (no port spec needs it): cross-backend COMMIT PREPARED, pg_prepared_xacts view, pg_twophase restart durability, detaching a prepared xact so the originating connection can run further transactions. | resolved | 2026-06-25 | M0118-0009 intra-grant-inplace (design 0118-0116) | LANDED enabler (NOT a promotion): permutation 9 (`b1 grant1 b3 sfu3 revoke4 c1 r3`) now byte-identical — first divergence advanced L206→L235 (now perm 10). Two gaps closed: (1) plpgsql body parser rejected a bare REVOKE inside revoke4's `DO $$ … $$` block — GRANT/REVOKE are not reserved keywords (SQL lexer keeps them as plain idents) so a leading REVOKE fell through to parseAssign → "expected ':=' or '=' after revoke" BEFORE the EXCEPTION WHEN others handler could run (goopg emitted an ERROR PG never raises). (2) sfu3's pg_class rowmark (`SELECT relhasindex FROM pg_class WHERE oid=…::regclass FOR UPDATE`) completed immediately instead of &lt;waiting&gt; behind grant1's uncommitted ACL change: refactored ddlOp.waitForTableACLChange into the free func executor.waitTableACLChange(ctx, tableOID) and made lockRowsOp.maybeRecordPgClassRowMark record the rowmark FIRST (so a peer REVOKE blocks behind us via waitForPgClassRowMarks — 0118-0114) THEN await the table's ACL xmax (PG order: acquire LockTuple, then await tuple xmax); the method now returns *ExecError propagated by drainAndStamp. | resolved | 2026-06-25 | M0118-0005 fk-partitioned-1 referenced-side (design 0118-0119) | LANDED enabler (NOT a promotion): closed the COMMITTED-attach Class B perms. After pfk1 is attached as a partition of FK-owner pfk (which references ppk), a DELETE FROM ppk1 (leaf of the *referenced* ppk) is now rejected referenced-side with `update or delete on table "ppk1" violates foreign key constraint "pfk_a_fkey_1" on table "pfk"`. Root cause: goopg's enforceFKOnDelete looks up via FindFKsReferencingTable("ppk1") but the FK's RefTable is the partitioned parent "ppk", so nothing matched and the delete passed silently. New executor.enforceFKOnDeletePartitionAncestor (called at end of enforceFKOnDelete) walks the deleted leaf's partition-ancestor chain (ppk1-&gt;ppk via new partitionParentTable helper), fires any FK referencing an ancestor, SKIPS per-partition FK clones (catalog.IsPartitionChild) so the violation names the ROOT pfk (scanTableForMatch on pfk already descends every partition), and appends the leaf's 1-based partition ordinal as the &lt;fkname&gt;_&lt;N&gt; dedup suffix (pfk_a_fkey_1) — same scheme as detachPartitionFKRefCheck (0118-0060). Two new map-backed catalog helpers IsPartitionChild + PartitionParentOf make partition identity reliable for ATTACH-created partitions, which leave Table.PartitionParentOID 0 (RegisterPartitionChild only updates the partitionChildren map). Also fixed the spec teardown `DROP TABLE ppk, pfk, pfk1` raising `table "pfk1" does not exist`: dropPartitionDescendants now records each cascade-dropped partition in the statement's cascadeDropped set (both qualified + bare name) so a partition listed alongside its parent is dropped once (PG resolves all DROP targets as one deduped set), mirroring the sibling inheritance cascade. Files: internal/executor/operators_fk.go (enforceFKOnDeletePartitionAncestor + partitionParentTable + enforceFKOnDelete call), internal/catalog/catalog.go (IsPartitionChild + PartitionParentOf), internal/executor/operators_ddl.go (dropPartitionDescendants cascadeDropped marking), docs/design/0118-0119 + README. Result: Class A (0118-0118) + the 4 committed-attach Class B perms byte-identical to PG 18.3; probe defer 130/133 lines — first divergence moved to the first CONCURRENT Class B perm (s1b s2b s2a s1d s2c s1c). Gates: probe Class A + committed Class B byte-exact; non-regression ReferentialIntegrity/RiTrigger/FkDeadlock/FkDeadlock2/FkContention/FkSnapshot/TemporalRangeIntegrity/PartitionConcurrentAttach/DetachPartitionConcurrently{1,2,3,4}/AlterTable4/InheritTemp/PartitionDropIndexLocking strict PASS; go test ./internal/executor/ ./internal/catalog/ PASS; go build ./... clean; pgbench smoke = pre-commit hook. | full promotion of fk-partitioned-1 (+ fk-partitioned-2) | CONCURRENT Class B (3 perms: s1b s2b s2a s1d s2c s1c, s2b s1b s2a s1d s2c s1c, s2b s2a s1b s1d s2c s1c) remains: s1d `delete from ppk1` must block `<waiting ...>` behind an UNCOMMITTED s2a attach and error once it commits. Needs the attach's referenced-row FOR-KEY-SHARE lock HELD TO COMMIT (cloneAndValidateAttachPartitionFKs / scanTableForMatchFKWait waits on in-flight deletes but leaves NO lock a later delete blocks on); during the uncommitted window IsPartitionChild(pfk1) is still false (RegisterPartitionChild deferred to commit) so the referenced-side check fires eagerly and mis-names the clone. NEW `quoteArrayElement` (catalog/catalog.go) reproduces array_out's element quoting; NEW `arrayTextLiteral` wraps elements as `{…}` quoting each; `optionsArrayLiteral` delegates to it (metachar-free elements stay byte-identical bare; `host 'a,b'` → `{"host=a,b"}`). The three pg_class.reloptions renderers (table/toast/index) also route through `arrayTextLiteral` for one authoritative quoter (their values are numeric/bool/enum-word so output is unchanged but the path is now metachar-safe). goopg's existing `parseTextArray` already unquotes `"host=a,b"` → `host=a,b`, which `pg_options_to_table` splits on the first `=` to recover `a,b`. | resolved | 2026-07-01 | M0119-0004 | DROP CAST type-name-synonym key fix (closes the loop #48 deferral, unrelated to the now-closed slice-389 restart-persistence backlog): `RegisterCast`/`DropCast`/`CastByTypes` (`internal/catalog/catalog.go`) keyed the cast registry on `lower(source)+"\x00"+lower(target)` — the raw parsed spelling — so `DROP CAST (real AS text)` failed to find a cast created via `CREATE CAST (float4 AS text) ...` (PG built-in type-name synonyms never cross-resolved in that key scheme). Fixed via new `castKey`/`castKeyTypeName` helpers that resolve each side through `TypeNameToOID` before keying, matching how `pg_cast`'s virtual view already renders `castsource`/`casttarget`. While landing this, found and fixed a second, previously-latent bug in the same code path: `TypeNameToOID` falls back to `OIDText` for any name it doesn't recognize (domains/enums/composites aren't in its builtin switch), so naively keying on its raw result would have collapsed every distinct user-defined-type cast into one shared `OIDText` bucket, letting unrelated casts silently overwrite each other. `castKeyTypeName` detects the fallback (`OIDText` result but input wasn't literally `"text"`) and keys on the lowercased name instead in that case. New tests: `internal/catalog/cast_synonym_test.go` (`TestDropCastResolvesTypeNameSynonyms`, `TestDropCastResolvesMultiWordSynonyms`, `TestRegisterCastIdempotentAcrossSynonyms`, `TestCastByTypesDistinctForUnrelatedTypes`, `TestCastByTypesDistinctForUnknownUserDefinedTypes`). | `CommentOnStmt`'s CAST comment resolution (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`, slice 396) calls the same `CastByTypes` so it inherits this fix automatically, but was not independently re-verified with a synonym-spelled `COMMENT ON CAST` in this loop. | resolved | 2026-07-01 | M0119-0004 | `regoperator`/`regprocedure` schema-qualification (loop #38, design `0119-0004-create-operator-roundtrip.md` "Loop #38", DU-002 slice 412): closes the loop #37 row's deferral (c). Confirmed root cause (`postgres/src/backend/utils/adt/regproc.c` `format_operator_extended`/`format_procedure_extended`): pg_dump's connection always runs `search_path=''` (`ALWAYS_SECURE_SEARCH_PATH_SQL`, applied in `setup_connection`, `pg_dump.c:1379`), so `OperatorIsVisible`/`ProcedureIsVisible` never find a non-`pg_catalog` object visible for that session — every such reference comes back schema-qualified; `pg_catalog` itself stays unqualified because PG always implicitly searches it regardless of `search_path`'s content (this is the one case loop #37's proposed "unconditionally qualify" fix would have gotten wrong — a builtin FUNCTION/OPERATOR entry, e.g. `dumpOpclass`'s own `btint4cmp`, must stay bare). New `catalog.RegprocedureNameAndSchema`/`(*InMemory).RegoperatorNameAndSchema` return `(schema, sig, ok)` alongside the existing bare-name functions (which now delegate to them): `"pg_catalog"` for a builtin, the routine's/operator's declared namespace (default `"public"`) for a user-defined one — with a `PublicNamespaceOID` special-case, since `NewInMemory`'s `schemas` map aliases both `"public"` and `"pg_toast"` to OID 2200 and a generic `SchemaNameForOID` reverse lookup would nondeterministically pick either (caught by an intermittent failure only when the full `internal/executor` suite ran, not the new test alone). New `executor.regObjectSchemaVisible(ctx, schema)` mirrors the real visibility rule (`pg_catalog` always visible; otherwise must appear in `searchPathSchemas(ctx)`, the pre-existing effective-search-path resolver) and gates qualification in both `regprocedure`/`regoperator` `CastExpr` branches (`internal/executor/expr.go`); `appendTypedCellText`'s direct-column-typed rendering (`internal/server/dispatch.go`) is deliberately left unchanged (no per-session context there, and `dumpOpclass`/`dumpOpfamily` never reach it — both always cast explicitly). Verified against a freshly-built, live PG 18.3 instance: the loop #37 fixture now dumps byte-identical (`OPERATOR 1 public.~=~(integer,integer)` qualified, `FUNCTION 1 (integer, integer) btint4cmp(integer,integer)` bare). Design `0117-0006-clog-slru-buffer-pool.md` status flipped to "Part A+B+C landed"; fix_plan M0117-0006 box checked. | Deliberate, accepted compatibility cut (not a PG-fidelity gap — PG itself has no such dual-store distinction, so this makes goopg's CLOG MORE faithful to PG's own single-SLRU-store model): a data dir last touched by a **pre-Part-B** binary could in principle carry committed/aborted bytes in the legacy flat file that were never projected into the SLRU (e.g. a crash between the old `flush()` write and the old `mirrorGroupToSLRULocked` SLRU write) — `OpenCLog` no longer reads `path` at all, so those bytes are now silently unrecoverable. | not a resume point requiring further engine work — justified because Part B already stopped trusting the flat file as the live store, and every gate/rollout in this project re-inits the data dir across a memory-model change of this kind (Hard-won Rule #3); recorded here only so a future loop doesn't rediscover this as a surprise if a pre-Part-B data dir is ever found in the wild. |  |

| resolved | 2026-07-02 | M0119-0004 | extended-protocol `SET ROLE`/`SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION` role-tracking LANDED (loop #63, design `0119-0004-create-operator-roundtrip.md` "Loop #63"): closes this row's own "`dispatch_extended.go`'s extended-protocol path has no `SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION`/`SET ROLE` role-tracking at all" item from the row above (loop #60). Live-probing the extended-query protocol (Parse/Bind/Execute/Sync) found the actual behavior was worse than a silent no-op: `internal/server/extended.go`'s `executeExtendedQuery` fast-path switch had no `SET ROLE`/`SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION`/`RESET ROLE`/`RESET SESSION AUTHORIZATION` cases, so those statements fell into the generic `SET `-prefix case and `sess.Set("role", ..., false)` errored `22023 unrecognized configuration parameter "ROLE"` (confirmed via a temporary revert + the new regression test — RED). Separately, `internal/parser/parser.go`'s `parseSet` unconditionally discarded the `SET ROLE` role name (`_, _ = p.parseIdent()`, forced `Default = true`) and `internal/executor/operators_utility_settings.go`'s `utilitySettingsOp` treated `SET ROLE`/`RESET ROLE` as unconditional no-ops (M0097-0071) — the SAME executor entry point multi-statement simple-query batches use, so `SET ROLE foo; TRUNCATE bar;` in one simple-query message was equally broken, not extended-protocol-specific as previously assumed. Fixed all three: parser now captures the role name into `SetStmt.Value`; new `executor.Context.SetRole` callback (sibling of the pre-existing `SetSessionAuthorization`) is invoked by `utilitySettingsOp`'s `"role"` cases and wired identically to `SetSessionAuthorization` in both `internal/server/dispatch.go` (simple-query executor path) and `internal/server/dispatch_extended.go` (extended-query executor path, previously entirely unwired); `extended.go`'s fast-path switch gained dedicated `SET ROLE`/`SET [LOCAL] SESSION AUTHORIZATION`/`RESET ROLE`/`RESET SESSION AUTHORIZATION` cases mirroring `server/query.go`'s `handleQuery`. `handleExecuteFrame`→`executeExtendedQuery`→`executeExtendedQueryViaExecutor` all gained a `connTx *connTxState` parameter, threaded from `server.go`'s `serveConn` (which already constructs `connTx` per connection). New `TestExtendedProtocolSetRoleTracksNonSuperuserRole` (`internal/server`) drives raw Parse/Bind/Execute/Sync frames and asserts `SHOW is_superuser` flips off/on identically to the simple-query behavior; parser subtests in `TestParseShowSetReset`. Gates: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean; `internal/parser`+`internal/executor`+`internal/server` suites PASS; `TestPort_IsolationTruncateConflict` PASS (no regression to the existing simple-query-protocol TRUNCATE-ownership spec); `TestPort_PgDumpConnectionSetup` PASS; TPC-H spotcheck Q12=2/Q13=33 PASS; pgbench smoke = pre-commit hook. | `SET LOCAL ROLE`/`SET LOCAL SESSION AUTHORIZATION` are parsed with `s.Local` set but neither the executor nor either wire-protocol path gives `LOCAL`'s transaction-scoped-revert semantics any special handling — the role change persists past the transaction boundary exactly like a non-`LOCAL` `SET ROLE` would (pre-existing limitation for `SESSION AUTHORIZATION`, now also true for `ROLE` by construction — not a new regression). `catalog.Subscription.Owner`/`Publication.Owner` non-bootstrap-role tracking and the `DBOID()`-vs-`FirstUserOID` split (both from the row above) remain open — untouched by this loop. | `SET LOCAL ROLE` transaction-scoped revert: would need `connTxState` to snapshot `NonSuperuserRole` at `SET LOCAL` time and restore it at COMMIT/ROLLBACK (mirrors PG's `GUC_ACTION_LOCAL` stack, `guc.c`); no forcing fixture exists today (isolation specs that use `SET ROLE` don't also nest it inside a transaction that then rolls back). `Subscription.Owner`/`Publication.Owner`/`DBOID()` split: see the resume points already recorded in the row above (loop #60), unchanged. |  |
| resolved | 2026-07-02 | M0119-0004 | `SET LOCAL ROLE`/`SET LOCAL SESSION AUTHORIZATION` transaction-scoped revert LANDED (loop #64, design `0119-0004-create-operator-roundtrip.md` "Loop #64"): closes this row's own "would need `connTxState` to snapshot `NonSuperuserRole` at `SET LOCAL` time and restore it at COMMIT/ROLLBACK" resume point from the row above (loop #63). Live repro against a real running server (psql, one statement per Simple Query message — the common client shape) surfaced a more severe, previously-undocumented bug with the SAME root cause: `SET LOCAL ROLE <name>` sent alone raised `unrecognized configuration parameter "role"` instead of doing anything, because `server/query.go`'s fast-path switch had a dedicated `"SET LOCAL SESSION AUTHORIZATION "` case but no analogous `"SET LOCAL ROLE "` case, so it fell through to the generic `"SET LOCAL "` handler which mis-parsed "ROLE <name>" as GUC name "role" (not a `config.Registry` variable — SET ROLE is tracked entirely via `connTx.NonSuperuserRole`) via `sess.Set`. The identical gap existed in `internal/server/extended.go`'s extended-protocol fast path (added loop #63, same missing case). Fixed both the routing bug and the revert semantics together (same call sites): new `connTxState.LocalRolePriorValue *string` + `SnapshotLocalRoleIfNeeded(local bool)` (`internal/server/conn_tx.go`) captures `NonSuperuserRole`'s pre-change value on the FIRST `SET LOCAL` within an active explicit transaction (a second `SET LOCAL` in the same transaction does not move the restore target, mirroring PostgreSQL's `GUC_ACTION_LOCAL` stack, `guc.c`); `End()` (the shared COMMIT/ROLLBACK teardown, called on every path including connection teardown) restores and clears it unconditionally. `executor.Context.SetSessionAuthorization`/`SetRole` gained a `local bool` parameter threaded from `operators_utility_settings.go`'s `stmt.Local`; the `dispatch.go`/`dispatch_extended.go` closures call `SnapshotLocalRoleIfNeeded` before mutating, and `EndLocalTransaction` (previously a bare `= sess.EndTransaction` alias) is now a closure that also re-syncs `is_superuser`/`ectx.NonSuperuserRole` from the just-restored `connTx.NonSuperuserRole`. New `"SET LOCAL ROLE "` fast-path cases added to both `query.go` and `extended.go` (mirroring the existing `"SET LOCAL SESSION AUTHORIZATION "` case) fix the routing bug; `extended.go`'s inline `"SET ROLE "` block was replaced by a new shared `setRoleFastPath` helper (sibling of the existing `setSessionAuthorizationFastPath`, both now `local`-aware). Verified live end-to-end: `SET LOCAL ROLE` alone no longer errors; reverts correctly at both COMMIT and ROLLBACK; two chained `SET LOCAL ROLE` calls in one transaction still revert to the pre-transaction value; plain (non-LOCAL) `SET ROLE` continues to persist past COMMIT unchanged. Tests: `internal/server/conn_tx_local_role_test.go` (unit-level snapshot/restore contract) + `internal/server/set_local_role_test.go` (full wire-protocol proof, one statement per message). Gates: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean; `internal/server`+`internal/executor` suites PASS; `go test -race -count=1 ./internal/wal/... ./internal/mvcc/...` PASS; `TestPort_IsolationTruncateConflict` PASS; `TestPort_PgDumpConnectionSetup` PASS; TPC-H spotcheck Q12=2/Q13=33 PASS; pgbench smoke = pre-commit hook. | Extended-protocol `BEGIN`/`COMMIT`/`ROLLBACK` are intercepted before reaching the executor and returned as no-op command tags (`dispatch_extended.go`'s `planner.Transaction` branch), so `connTx.active` never becomes true from a purely extended-protocol transaction — `SET LOCAL ROLE` issued entirely over the extended protocol still has no transaction boundary to revert at (behaves like a plain `SET`). This is the pre-existing, already-documented "extended protocol is auto-commit-per-statement" architectural limitation ([[goopg_extended_protocol_autocommit]]), not a new gap, and was out of scope for this loop. `catalog.Subscription.Owner`/`Publication.Owner` non-bootstrap-role tracking and the `DBOID()`-vs-`FirstUserOID` split (both from the loop #60 row) remain open, untouched. | Extended-protocol `SET LOCAL ROLE` revert: would need `dispatch_extended.go`'s `planner.Transaction` branch to actually call `connTx.Begin`/`connTx.End` for BEGIN/COMMIT/ROLLBACK reaching the extended protocol instead of treating them as no-ops — a substantially larger change (the "extended protocol has no explicit-txn model" architectural entanglement already on record) with no forcing fixture today (no isolation spec drives BEGIN/COMMIT purely over Parse/Bind/Execute/Sync). `Subscription.Owner`/`Publication.Owner`/`DBOID()` split: see the resume points already recorded in the loop #60 row, unchanged. |  |

| resolved | 2026-07-02 | M0119-0004 | Event trigger CREATE/DROP/ALTER WAL/restart persistence LANDED (loop #71, design `0119-0004-create-operator-roundtrip.md` "Loop #71"): closes the loop #70 row's own WAL-persistence resume point, resolving that row. Five new WAL record kinds (`RecordKindCreateEventTrigger`/`DropEventTrigger`/`AlterEventTriggerEnabled`/`AlterEventTriggerRename`/`AlterEventTriggerOwner`, kinds 56-60, `internal/wal/recovery.go`) mirror the PubSub pattern (loop #68); new post-replay driver `internal/initdb/event_trigger_ddl_recovery.go` + five `*DuringRecovery` catalog mutators (`internal/catalog/catalog.go`) + new `LookupEventTrigger` accessor; the three executor mutation sites (`execCreateEventTrigger`/`execAlterEventTrigger`/`execDropCompat`'s `"event trigger"` case, `internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`) now WAL-log before returning success. Broader `CREATE FUNCTION` WAL/restart persistence: needs its own investigation of `internal/catalog.InMemory`'s routines registry + a new WAL record kind family (likely largest of the DU-002 restart-persistence follow-ups so far, since routines carry a full function body/language/arg-type list, not just a handful of scalar fields) — no resume-point file/function pinned yet, first needs a scoping pass over `RegisterRoutine`-equivalent call sites in `internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`. | CREATE FUNCTION persistence is a pre-existing gap far broader than the single-loop event-trigger WAL slice; recording as a fresh discovery rather than expanding this loop's scope, consistent with how PubSub/aggregate/collation WAL persistence landed as separate loops from their own CREATE/DROP work. |
| resolved | 2026-07-02 | M0119-0004 | `CREATE`/`ALTER`/`DROP FUNCTION` and `CREATE`/`ALTER PROCEDURE` WAL/restart persistence LANDED (loop #73, design `0119-0004-create-operator-roundtrip.md` "Loop #73"): closes the loop #71 row's own resume point (the "newly discovered while verifying live" `CREATE FUNCTION` persistence gap), resolving that row. Four new WAL record kinds (`RecordKindCreateFunction`/`DropFunction`/`AlterFunctionRename`/`AlterFunctionFlags`, kinds 61-64, `internal/wal/recovery.go`) mirror the event-trigger pattern (loop #71) with one structural difference: `CreateFunctionPayload`/`FunctionArgPayload` are struct-based (too many fields — full arg list with names/types/modes/defaults, return type, body, 8+ scalar attributes — for a flat positional Encode/Decode signature) and `Body` gets a 4-byte length prefix instead of the usual 2-byte one since a plpgsql body can plausibly exceed 65535 bytes. `DropFunction` carries the resolved OID (not name+signature) since DROP FUNCTION's own overload resolution already happened live — replaying by OID sidesteps re-resolving a possibly-ambiguous bare name against a partially-replayed registry. New `catalog.Routines` recovery mutators (`CreateDuringRecovery`/`DropByOIDDuringRecovery`/`RenameByOIDDuringRecovery`/`SetFlagsByOIDDuringRecovery`, `internal/catalog/routines.go`) plus new post-replay driver `internal/initdb/function_ddl_recovery.go` (`replayFunctionDDLRecords`, wired into `open.go` right after the event-trigger replay call). `executor.extractRoutineDeps` was exported to `ExtractRoutineDeps` (one call site) so the recovery driver can recompute a SQL-language routine's `SequenceDeps`/`RoutineCallOIDs`/`TableDeps`/`ColumnDeps` from the replayed `Body`/`ArgDefaults` post-registration, the same way live `CREATE FUNCTION` derives them — these derived fields are deliberately NOT WAL-serialized. A transactional-correctness subtlety the event-trigger precedent didn't have to face: `DROP FUNCTION` has TWO removal paths (autocommit-immediate via `execDropFunction`'s `else` branch, and deferred-to-COMMIT via `ApplyDeferredRoutineDrops` for an explicit-transaction drop, per M0118-0009 `stats`) plus CASCADE-dependent drops (`functionsDependingOnRoutineOID`, dropped immediately regardless of transaction state, no rollback-undo). All three are WAL-logged at the point the removal is actually durable/unconditional — the autocommit branch and CASCADE block log immediately (no rollback-undo tracking exists for either, so immediate logging can't diverge from a later ROLLBACK), while the deferred branch logs *inside* `ApplyDeferredRoutineDrops` itself (which only ever runs at real COMMIT — `operators_tx.go execCommit` and `dispatch.go`'s simple-query `TxCommit` case — never at ROLLBACK, which discards the deferred entries via `TakeDeferredRoutineDrops` without calling it), making the log point inherently rollback-safe rather than a copy-paste of the event-trigger "log at statement time" convention. Verified via a real `goopg stop`/`start` restart cycle against the same data dir (not just unit tests): `CREATE FUNCTION` (with a DEFAULT arg and numeric(10,2) typmod) + `CREATE FUNCTION IMMUTABLE` + `CREATE PROCEDURE` + a `DROP FUNCTION` all survive one restart; the dropped function and the pre-rename old name are correctly absent; `ALTER FUNCTION RENAME TO` + `ALTER FUNCTION STRICT SECURITY DEFINER` survive with the renamed name and all four flags intact; `CALL` on the restored procedure and a call on the restored function both still execute correctly against the recovered `Body`. Tests: `internal/wal/function_ddl_test.go` (encode/decode round trips incl. numeric(10,2) typmod + multi-byte UTF-8 + wrong-kind/truncated-payload guards across all 4 kinds), `internal/initdb/function_ddl_recovery_test.go` (6 tests: real `Init`/`Open`/`WAL.Append`/`Close`/re-`Open` round trips for CREATE, CREATE-OR-REPLACE-overwrites-in-place-preserving-OID, CREATE+DROP, CREATE+RENAME+FLAGS, plus missing-WAL-dir/nil-catalog no-op guards). Gates: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean; `internal/wal`+`internal/catalog`+`internal/executor`+`internal/initdb`+`internal/planner`+`internal/parser`+`internal/server` suites PASS; `TestPort_PgDumpConnectionSetup` PASS; TPC-H spotcheck Q12=2/Q13=33 PASS; full pre-commit gate incl. pgbench TPC-B smoke (0 failed transactions across all 3 workloads) PASS. | `DROP PROCEDURE` has NO WAL persistence — it mutates the registry immediately (`execDropProcedure`, unconditional, no explicit-transaction deferral) but is undoable via `ROLLBACK` through a *different* mechanism than `DROP FUNCTION` (`BasicSession.pendingRoutineDrops`/`AddPendingRoutineDrop`, restored via `rs.Create(r, true)` at 7 scattered call sites across `dispatch.go`/`server.go`/`twophase.go`, not the single `ApplyDeferredRoutineDrops` chokepoint `DROP FUNCTION` funnels through). Logging at `execDropProcedure`'s immediate-mutation point the way CREATE/ALTER do would be UNSAFE — a `BEGIN; DROP PROCEDURE p(); ROLLBACK;` would durably WAL a drop that the live session correctly un-does, so a post-restart server would show `p` gone even though it was rolled back. Deferred rather than shipped-unsafely. Also unchanged (pre-existing, not newly introduced): `CREATE FUNCTION`/`CREATE PROCEDURE` themselves still have no rollback-undo tracking at all (a `BEGIN; CREATE FUNCTION f(); ROLLBACK;` leaves `f` registered, live and post-restart alike — WAL logging stays *consistent* with that pre-existing in-memory behavior, not diverging from it). | `internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`'s `execDropProcedure` (~line 10650) needs its own commit-time WAL hook before it can log safely — either (a) give it a dedicated "log at commit" list mirroring `ApplyDeferredRoutineDrops`'s single chokepoint (would need touching the same scattered `TakePendingRoutineDrops`-draining call sites this loop deliberately avoided), or (b) unify `DROP PROCEDURE`'s transactional model onto the same `TakeDeferredRoutineDrops`/`ApplyDeferredRoutineDrops` path `DROP FUNCTION` already uses (would also fix a pre-existing, unrelated transactional-visibility bug: today a concurrent session sees a `DROP PROCEDURE`'d procedure disappear before the dropping transaction even commits, since `Routines` has no MVCC and the immediate `rs.DropRoutine` call is visible cluster-wide instantly). Option (b) is likely the better fix — it closes two gaps in one motion — but changes live transactional behavior, so it deserves its own scoped loop rather than folding into a WAL-persistence slice. | bounding a single loop to the two DROP FUNCTION paths sharing one well-established chokepoint (`ApplyDeferredRoutineDrops`) plus the always-immediate CASCADE block (no undo tracking, safe to log eagerly); `DROP PROCEDURE`'s scattered 7-call-site undo mechanism is a materially different, larger surface that risks a genuine WAL/ROLLBACK divergence bug if rushed. |
| resolved | 2026-07-02 | M0119-0004 | `DROP PROCEDURE` WAL/restart persistence LANDED (loop #74, design `0119-0004-create-operator-roundtrip.md` "Loop #74"): closes the loop #73 row's own resume point via option (b) — unify `DROP PROCEDURE` onto the exact `DeferredRoutineDrop`/`ApplyDeferredRoutineDrops` mechanism `DROP FUNCTION` already uses, instead of a parallel commit-time hook. `execDropProcedure` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`) already resolved the target routine before mutating anything, so only its final drop step changed: inside an explicit transaction it now calls `bsess.AddDeferredRoutineDrop(...)` (identical to `execDropFunction`'s branch, procedure stays resolvable/callable until COMMIT) instead of an immediate `DropRoutine` + `AddPendingRoutineDrop`; autocommit still drops immediately but now also calls `walLogDropRoutine` (previously DROP-FUNCTION-only — its doc comment updated). `ApplyDeferredRoutineDrops` and the WAL replay driver (`internal/initdb/function_ddl_recovery.go`, `RecordKindDropFunction` → `DropByOIDDuringRecovery`) needed ZERO code changes — both are already OID-keyed and kind-agnostic between function and procedure, only their header comments were updated. This closes two gaps in one motion, exactly as the loop #73 row anticipated: the missing WAL persistence, AND the pre-existing transactional-visibility bug where a concurrent session saw a `DROP PROCEDURE`'d name vanish before the dropping transaction committed (goopg's shared `Routines` registry has no per-session MVCC — visibility depends entirely on *when* the mutation happens). The now-fully-dead `BasicSession.pendingRoutineDrops`/`AddPendingRoutineDrop`/`TakePendingRoutineDrops` mechanism (its only caller was `execDropProcedure`) was deleted outright across all 7 call sites (`operators_tx.go` execCommit clear + execRollback restore-loop, `dispatch.go` ×5 restore-loops/clear, `server.go` `rollbackOpenTxnOnTeardown`, `twophase.go` `abortForPrepareSSIFailure`) rather than left as dead code — safe because a deferred drop's ROLLBACK needs no restore action (the registry was never mutated), and every one of those sites already calls `EndExplicitTransaction`, which unconditionally resets `deferRoutineDrops = nil`. Tests: `TestExecDropProcedureRemovesEntry`/`TestExecDropProcedureDeferredToCommit`/`TestExecDropProcedureRollbackLeavesEntry` (`internal/executor/operators_function_test.go`). Verified via a real `goopg stop`/`start` restart cycle against a dedicated data dir (`tmp/loop74-verify`, port 5536): `BEGIN; DROP PROCEDURE p1(int); ROLLBACK;` leaves `p1` callable; the same DROP + `COMMIT` removes it; an autocommit `DROP PROCEDURE p2(int)` and a third procedure `p3` (create+drop, autocommit) are also gone; all three stay absent after `goopg stop`/`start`, while an undropped `keep_me` procedure survives the same restart and still executes via `CALL`. Gates: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean; `internal/executor`+`internal/server`+`internal/wal`+`internal/catalog`+`internal/initdb`+`internal/parser`+`internal/planner` suites PASS; `TestPort_PgDumpConnectionSetup` PASS; TPC-H spotcheck Q12=2/Q13=33 PASS; full pre-commit gate incl. pgbench TPC-B smoke (0 failed transactions across all 3 workloads) PASS. | None — the `CREATE`/`ALTER`/`DROP FUNCTION`/`PROCEDURE` surface goopg supports at the SQL level is now entirely WAL-persisted; no residual scope remains under this ledger row. | n/a (fully resolved this loop) | n/a |
| resolved | 2026-07-02 | M0119-0004 | RESOLVED same day (root-0022, interactive session): root cause was NOT missing TOAST (goopg has had real TOAST since M0046-0006) but `executor.toastOIDCounter` — a process-local, non-persisted `atomic.Int64` — resetting to 0 on every restart while the TOAST relation it writes `chunk_id` rows into survives on disk; the first TOASTed value written after any restart (no crash needed) reissued `chunk_id 1`, colliding with a pre-restart value's still-resident `chunk_id 1` in the same table's TOAST relation, and `DetoastValue`'s oid-only scan spliced the two unrelated values' bytes together. Fix: new `executor.SeedToastOIDCounter`/`MaxToastChunkIDInRel`/`AdvanceToastOIDCounterPast` (`internal/executor/toast.go`) scan every user table's TOAST relation once at startup (wired into `internal/initdb/open.go` right after the existing M0106-0013 catalog-OID-advance loop, unconditionally — even on the M0114 cache-hit path) and advance the counter past the max `chunk_id` found, mirroring the established catalog-OID restart pattern. `MaxToastChunkIDInRel` short-circuits via `Pool.Exists` before touching `NBlocks`/`Pin` to avoid the smgr O_CREATE-recreates-removed-files pitfall. Verified: `TestToastOIDCounterCollisionAcrossRestart` (executor-level, confirmed to FAIL with byte-exact reproduction of the reported corruption when the reseed call is removed) + `TestMaxToastChunkIDInRelNoFile` + `TestSeedToastOIDCounterAdvancesPastExisting`, plus a real-cluster-restart e2e `TestPort_ToastValueSurvivesRestartWithoutCollision` (`internal/testport/toast_oid_restart_durability_test.go`, mirrors `serial_sequence_durability_test.go`). Full `internal/executor`+`internal/initdb`+`internal/storage` suites pass; TPC-H spotcheck and pgbench smoke pending final verification this loop. Design `docs/design/root-0022-toast-oid-restart-durability.md`. A narrower, secondary durability gap (TOAST chunk writes not WAL/FPI-protected per-insert, only fires on an unclean crash before the next checkpoint) was found during investigation and is recorded as its own new ledger row below rather than folded into this fix. Original row text follows. — Nothing landed for this row — recording a fresh corruption discovery from the WordPress workload (interactive session, 2026-07-02). | Oversized-row cold-restart corruption: a ~30KB `wp_options.option_value` (theme-patterns transient; goopg has no TOAST) was stored and readable while pages were warm, but after a later cold start the NEIGHBORING row `wp_user_roles` read back with a 3992-byte prefix of the 30KB transient as its value (and the page's rows also showed NULL option_id from the pre-fix serial gap) — i.e. checkpoint/replay of a page family holding an oversized tuple corrupted adjacent tuples' varlena data. Site died with `array_keys(): Argument #1 must be of type array` in WP_Roles until repaired by deleting the transients + resetting wp_user_roles. | RECURRED deterministically on a second fresh WordPress install (same session, all serial/default fixes in): after warm dashboard traffic wrote three oversized options (30181/22748/11356 bytes) and a clean stop/start, `wp_user_roles` again read back with its OWN varlena length (3992 both incidents) but ANOTHER row's bytes (`a:2:{s:4:"hash";...` — the styles transient) — i.e. the line pointer resolves to shifted/foreign tuple bytes on the cold path, pointing at a prune/compaction vs on-disk line-pointer divergence (see goopg_two_heap_prune_paths) rather than raw torn pages. TWO synthetic repros came back NEGATIVE: (a) small rows + 30KB row, restart, big-row UPDATE by unique name; (b) same via `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` refresh + double restart — so the trigger needs WordPress's real churn (repeated option upserts + opportunistic pruning on the same page family). Next repro attempt should drive hundreds of mixed-size option upserts (mimicking wp-cron/admin traffic) between restarts, then diff all rows byte-for-byte. Start at the oversized-tuple write path (line-pointer/continuation handling in `internal/storage` PageAddHeapTuple callers) and the checkpoint page-write path; goopg lacks TOAST (`postgres/src/backend/access/common/toast_internals.c` is the upstream mechanism). | Discovered while verifying the coercion fix on the live WordPress instance; a TOAST-class storage fix is a major subsystem effort, far out of scope for the session's three slices. Not yet minimally reproduced — the repro sketch above is the concrete next step. |

After a restart, `INSERT` omitting a defaulted column inserts NULL instead of the default (NOT NULL violation when constrained). | resolved | 2026-07-02 | M0119-0004 | RESOLVED same day (root-0022 follow-up, interactive session, loop #2): closes the row above's own resume point. `writeHeapTupleToRel` (`internal/executor/toast.go`) now routes both its append-to-existing-page and PinNew-new-page branches through `markHeapInsertDirty(ctx.Pool, slot, ctx.Pool.LogHeapInsert(), rel, blk, lineSlot, raw)` — the exact helper the main heap-insert path uses (`operators_storage.go:7750`) — instead of a bare `ctx.Pool.MarkDirty(slot)`. `markHeapInsertDirty` calls `Pool.MarkDirtyLogicalChange`, which unconditionally invokes the per-insert WAL emitter on every call (epoch-gating only controls whether an *additional* FPI also fires), so every TOAST chunk row is now independently WAL-durable regardless of whether an earlier chunk already dirtied the same page this checkpoint epoch. The previously-dead `raw`/`blk` locals in `writeHeapTupleToRel` (marked `_ = raw`/`_ = blk`, a leftover scaffold) are now the WAL record's tuple-bytes/block-number inputs. No format change: replay is the plain `RecordKindHeapInsert` redo record, generic by RelFileNode/block/line-slot; logical decoding is unaffected since `pgoutput.Change` already filters out any relation absent from the publication snapshot, and TOAST relations (synthetic `RelOid = mainRel.RelOid + 100_000_000`) were never registered as publishable tables. Test: `internal/executor/toast_test.go` `TestToastChunkInsertsAreIndividuallyWALLogged` — wires a real `LogHeapInsert`/`LogPageImage` pool (mirrors `internal/initdb/open.go` production wiring), writes a 3-chunk value landing on one TOAST page, asserts the WAL hook fires 3 times (was 0 on the pre-fix code, confirmed by reverting the fix and re-running). | resolved | 2026-07-03 | M0110-0001 | Plain-`INHERITS` NOT NULL constraint locality fix LANDED (design `docs/design/0110-0001-pg-dump-tap-port.md` "Plain-`INHERITS` NOT NULL constraint locality fix (loop #48)"): live `pg_dump` probing (not sourced from a prior ledger row — discovery via a fresh fixture, `CREATE TABLE parent_t (id int PRIMARY KEY, name text); CREATE TABLE child_t (extra text) INHERITS (parent_t);`) found goopg's `pg_dump --schema-only` emitted a malformed `CREATE TABLE public.child_t ( NOT NULL id, extra text ) INHERITS (public.parent_t);` where live PostgreSQL 18.3 (verified side-by-side, `postgres/local_install`) emits `CREATE TABLE public.child_t (extra text) INHERITS (public.parent_t);` with no mention of `id` at all. Root cause: `execCreateTable`'s NOT NULL constraint registration loop (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go` ~line 3189, DU-002 slice 50) called `tbl.AddNotNull(..., isLocal=true, inhCount=0)` unconditionally for every NOT NULL column, even one carried in purely by `INHERITS` (`col.Inherited=true`, DU-002 slice 170) — making `pg_constraint.conislocal='t'` for `child_t.id`, which is exactly the condition `pg_dump.c`'s dumpTableSchema (~line 17213) uses to re-emit a non-printed inherited column as a standalone non-conforming `NOT NULL <col>` table element. Fixed: when `col.Inherited && s.PartitionOf == nil`, derive `isLocal`/`inhCount`/constraint-name from the `INHERITS` parent's own NOT NULL constraint on the same column (mirrors real PG's `MergeAttributes`). New `TestCreateTableInheritsNotNullConstraintIsNonLocal` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl_named_check_test.go`). Gates: build/vet clean; `internal/executor`+`internal/catalog`+`internal/parser`+`internal/server`+`internal/initdb` suites PASS; `TestPort_PgDumpConnectionSetup`+`TestPort_PgDump001Basic` PASS; live `pg_dump` smoke vs. a scratch real-PG-18.3 instance; `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS; pgbench smoke = pre-commit hook. | A **separate, pre-existing** bug found while confirming `PARTITION OF` is unaffected (not regressed) by this fix: a `CREATE TABLE child PARTITION OF parent FOR VALUES ...` child registers **zero** `pg_constraint` contype='n' rows for its inherited-NOT-NULL columns at all (confirmed via `psql`: `attnotnull='t'`/`attinhcount=1` on `pg_attribute`, but no matching `pg_constraint` row whatsoever), where live PG 18.3 gives it one with `conislocal='f'/coninhcount=1` reusing the parent's constraint name — so goopg's `pg_dump` of a real partition child currently omits the `CONSTRAINT <name> NOT NULL` inline suffix PG emits (`id integer,` instead of `id integer CONSTRAINT parent_id_not_null NOT NULL,`). Deliberately NOT touched by this loop's `s.PartitionOf == nil` guard (scoped to the plain-`INHERITS` case that was actually verified live against real PG). | `internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`'s NOT NULL constraint registration loop (~line 3189, same function, `execCreateTable`) needs a `s.PartitionOf != nil` branch that ALSO registers a NOT NULL constraint row for an inherited NOT NULL column, reusing the parent's name/deriving `inhCount` — likely very similar to the fix just landed, but needs its own live-PG-verified test (partition column-list printing is `ispartition`-gated differently in `pg_dump.c`, so the exact expected inline form — bare `NOT NULL` vs `CONSTRAINT <name> NOT NULL` — should be confirmed against a live PG partition example before implementing, not assumed from the plain-INHERITS case). | Scoped to the one confirmed-against-live-PG divergence this loop actually probed and fixed (ONE task per loop); the partition case is a distinct code path (a different `s.PartitionOf != nil` branch of the same function) that deserves its own live-PG verification pass rather than a same-loop copy-paste fix. |
| resolved | 2026-07-03 | M0110-0001 | `CREATE TABLE ... PARTITION OF` NOT NULL constraint locality fix LANDED (design `docs/design/0110-0001-pg-dump-tap-port.md` "PARTITION OF NOT NULL constraint locality fix"): resolves the follow-up gap recorded in the previous row. `execCreatePartitionChild` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`) previously called `tbl.AddNotNull` ONLY for columns in `poc.NotNullColumns` (the partition's own `(col NOT NULL)` column-override list), so a column NOT NULL purely because the partitioned parent enforces it got ZERO `pg_constraint` contype='n' row at all (confirmed live against `postgres/local_install` PG 18.3 and now byte-identical: `p_id_not_null`/`conislocal=f`/`coninhcount=1`, reusing the parent's name, exactly as real PG's `MergeAttributes` produces, vs. goopg's prior no-row). Fixed by iterating every NOT NULL `tbl.Columns` entry: a column absent from `poc.NotNullColumns` but present in `parent.NotNullConstraints` now reuses the parent's constraint name with `isLocal=false, inhCount=1`; a column explicitly in `poc.NotNullColumns` gets its own `<child>_<col>_not_null` name with `isLocal=true`, and `inhCount` is 1 if the parent ALSO enforces it (verified live: `p3a_id_not_null`/`t`/`1`) or 0 if the parent column is nullable (verified live: `p2a_v_not_null`/`t`/`0`) — three live-PG-verified sub-cases, not assumed. `pg_dump --schema-only` output confirmed byte-identical to real PG 18.3 for all three (`p1`: `id integer CONSTRAINT p_id_not_null NOT NULL,`; `p2a`/`p3a`: bare `NOT NULL` since the generated name matches PG's default-name-suppression rule). New `TestCreatePartitionChildNotNullConstraintLocality` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl_named_check_test.go`). Gates: build/vet clean; `internal/executor` suite PASS; `internal/catalog`+`internal/parser`+`internal/server`+`internal/initdb` PASS; `TestPort_PgDumpConnectionSetup` PASS; live `pg_dump`/`psql` smoke vs. a scratch real-PG-18.3 instance AND a scratch goopg instance (`bin/goopg init`/`start` on a throwaway data dir, port 5537) side by side. | While verifying, found a **separate, pre-existing, sibling** locality bug: for the PLAIN `INHERITS` path (not `PARTITION OF`), `execCreateTable`'s existing fix (previous ledger row, loop #48) leaves `coninhcount=0` for a column that is BOTH explicitly re-declared NOT NULL in the child's own column list AND already NOT NULL in an `INHERITS` parent (e.g. `CREATE TABLE child2 (id int NOT NULL, ...) INHERITS (parent)` where `parent.id` is already NOT NULL) — live PG 18.3 gives `coninhcount=1` in this "merging column ... with inherited definition" case (confirmed live: `child2_t_id_not_null`/`conislocal=t`/`coninhcount=1`), but goopg's existing (and existing-tested — see `TestCreateTableInheritsNotNullConstraintIsNonLocal`'s `child2NC.InhCount != 0` assertion) behavior gives `coninhcount=0`, because `col.Inherited` is false for a column present in the child's OWN column list (the `if col.Inherited && s.PartitionOf == nil` re-derivation branch never fires for it), so it falls through to the loop's `inhCount := 0` default. This does NOT affect `pg_dump` schema output (PG's `print_notnull` for a plain-INHERITS child only gates on `conislocal`, never `coninhcount`), so no dump-text divergence was introduced or exists here — it is a pure `pg_constraint.coninhcount` catalog-fidelity gap, visible only to a direct `pg_constraint` query (e.g. `pg_upgrade`/introspection tooling, not `pg_dump`). | `internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`'s `execCreateTable` NOT NULL registration loop (~line 3210, same `col.Inherited && s.PartitionOf == nil` block) needs a second case: when a column IS in the child's own explicit column list (`col.Inherited == false`) AND ALSO matches a NOT NULL constraint on an `INHERITS` parent, set `inhCount = 1` (still `isLocal = true`, still the child's own auto-generated name — only `inhCount` is wrong today). Mirrors the analogous case just fixed for `PARTITION OF` (`poc.NotNullColumns` + `parentNC != nil` → `inhCount = 1`) in this same loop's commit. Update `TestCreateTableInheritsNotNullConstraintIsNonLocal`'s `child2NC` assertion from `InhCount != 0` to `InhCount != 1` once fixed. | Out of scope for this loop (ONE task per loop): this loop's scope was the `PARTITION OF` gap named in the prior ledger row; the plain-`INHERITS` sibling gap is a distinct code path discovered only while writing this loop's live-PG verification queries, not something the prior row flagged. | resolved | 2026-07-03 | M0119-0004/M0110-0001 | loop #69: CREATE OPERATOR CLASS/FAMILY + pg_amop/pg_amproc restart/WAL persistence LANDED (design `docs/design/0119-0004-create-operator-roundtrip.md` "Loop #69 — CREATE OPERATOR CLASS/FAMILY + pg_amop/pg_amproc restart/WAL persistence (closes the loop #65/#66 \"still open\" item (1))") — closes the loop #65/#66 rows' own "still open" item (1). New WAL kinds 85–91 (`internal/wal/recovery.go`) mirror `RecordKindCreateOperator`; `RecordKindCreateAmOpMember`/`RecordKindCreateAmProcMember` cover both a `CREATE OPERATOR CLASS ... AS` list entry and an `ALTER OPERATOR FAMILY ... ADD` entry (shared `registerOpClassMembers` executor helper). New catalog `*DuringRecovery` counterparts + new driver `internal/initdb/operator_class_ddl_recovery.go`. **Two bugs found and fixed while landing this** (both documented in the design doc's own sections): (1) recovery must repopulate the legacy `opClassSchemas` map (`HasOpClass`'s backing store), not just the newer `userOperatorClasses` registry, or a post-restart `DROP OPERATOR CLASS` 42704s on a class `pg_dump` still shows; (2) `wal.ApplyRecord` had zero `case` arms for four *pre-existing* record kinds (79/80 GRANT/REVOKE ROLE membership, 83/84 CREATE/DROP OPERATOR from loop #65) — a **crash restart** (kill -KILL, no shutdown checkpoint) with one of these records still after the last checkpoint would hit `ApplyRecord`'s `default: unsupported kind N` fatal error and abort the entire WAL replay, refusing to start. Every prior "verified end-to-end incl. full restart" claim used a graceful `Close()` (always takes a shutdown checkpoint, which retroactively trims these catalog-only records out of the post-checkpoint replay input by accident, masking the gap). Fixed with two new no-op `case` arms. Live-verified with a deliberate `kill -KILL` restart (not graceful stop): family/class/AS-list `pg_amproc` member all survive; `DROP OPERATOR CLASS` post-restart succeeds and purges the member row; `pg_dump` round-trips correctly. Fix: new `(*InMemory) defaultUserBtreeOpclassForSubtype(subtypeOID) (uint32, bool)` (`internal/catalog/catalog.go`) scans `ListUserOperatorClasses()` for a `Method == btree`, `InTypeOID == subtypeOID`, `IsDefault == true` row; `resolveRangeOpclass`'s empty-`opclassName` branch tries the curated builtin map first (unchanged — pure optimization, it never varies at runtime) and falls back to this new method before raising `42704`. | resolved | 2026-07-03 | M0119-0004-ACLHEAP | loop #78: `TIME ZONE`/`SCHEMA`/`NAMES`/`ROLE`/`SESSION AUTHORIZATION`/`XML OPTION` special-form GUC translation for `ALTER DATABASE`/`ALTER ROLE ... SET` LANDED (design `docs/design/0119-0004-database-config-set-pgdump.md` "Follow-up: ... special-form GUC translation (loop #78)"), closing the `SET TIME ZONE`/`SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION` residual named by the `0119-0004bt`/`0119-0004bu` design-doc rows (loops #17 and the interactive-session `ALTER ROLE ... SET` follow-up). Real PG's `set_rest` grammar production (`postgres/src/backend/parser/gram.y` ~line 1708) accepts six special syntaxes as alternatives to the generic `name TO|= value` form — `TIME ZONE zone_value`, `SCHEMA Sconst`, `NAMES opt_encoding`, `ROLE NonReservedWord_or_Sconst`, `SESSION AUTHORIZATION {NonReservedWord_or_Sconst|DEFAULT}`, `XML OPTION {DOCUMENT|CONTENT}` — each translating to a plain GUC name/value pair (`timezone`/`search_path`/`client_encoding`/`role`/`session_authorization`/`xmloption`); `SetResetClause` (shared by plain `SET` and `AlterDatabaseSetStmt`/`AlterRoleSetStmt`) reduces to the identical `set_rest` production, so all six are valid inside `ALTER DATABASE`/`ALTER ROLE ... SET` too, and `dbcommands.c AlterDatabaseSet` -> `pg_db_role_setting.c AlterSetting` -> `guc_funcs.c ExtractSetVariableArgs` stores the translated pair exactly like the generic form. `parseAlterDatabaseConfig`/`parseAlterRoleConfig` (`internal/server/database_ddl.go`/`role_ddl.go`) only ever matched `configName TO|= value`/`RESET [name|ALL]`, so e.g. `SET TIME ZONE 'UTC'` treated `TIME` as the config name, found no `TO `/`=` immediately after, and silently fell through to the pre-existing no-op absorption — `CommandComplete` `ALTER DATABASE` was sent but nothing was applied, for commands a WordPress/ORM client can plausibly send (`SET SCHEMA`/`SET NAMES` are common compatibility idioms), not a hypothetical edge case. New shared helper `parseSetRestSpecialForm` (`internal/server/database_ddl.go`) recognizes the six forms on the raw text following `SET ` and returns the translated `(configName, configValue, reset)` before the generic parse runs; called from both `parseAlterDatabaseConfig` and `parseAlterRoleConfig`. `TRANSACTION SNAPSHOT` deliberately excluded — its `VAR_SET_MULTI` kind has no case in `ExtractSetVariableArgs`, so real PG cannot store it via `AlterSetting` either (a transaction-scoped command, not a persistable GUC). Live-verified end-to-end (throwaway data dir, real `psql`/`pg_dumpall` 18.3, full server restart cycle not needed since this reuses the already-restart-durable `pg_db_role_setting` WAL path): `ALTER DATABASE postgres SET TIME ZONE 'UTC'`/`SET SCHEMA 'app'`/`SET NAMES 'utf8'` all populate `pg_db_role_setting.setconfig` with the correct translated GUC name (`{timezone=UTC,search_path=app,client_encoding=utf8}`), and `ALTER ROLE verifyrole SET SESSION AUTHORIZATION 'postgres'` round-trips byte-identically through `pg_dumpall --roles-only` (`ALTER ROLE verifyrole SET session_authorization TO 'postgres';`). Tests: `TestParseAlterDatabaseConfig`/`TestParseAlterRoleConfig` (`internal/server/database_ddl_test.go`/`role_config_test.go`) extended with cases for all six forms plus one `IN DATABASE` combination. Gates: `go build ./...` clean; `internal/server` suite PASS (full package, no regression); live `psql`/`pg_dumpall` smoke as described; `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (Q12=2/Q13=33); pgbench smoke = pre-commit hook. | `SET ... FROM CURRENT` (reads the session's *current* GUC value rather than a literal — needs the session's live GUC lookup threaded through, which the six forms above did not need) remains unrecognised by both parse functions and falls through to no-op absorption, same as before this loop. All other standing M0119-0004-ACLHEAP residuals (extended-protocol-only dispatch, multi-database scope, `pg_dumpall` cluster-wide-dump surface untested, `ALTER ROLE ALL SET ...` unsupported) are unchanged, not addressed this loop. | `internal/server/database_ddl.go`'s `parseSetRestSpecialForm` would need a seventh case: `strings.HasPrefix(lower, "... from current")`-shaped detection reading `s.cfg`'s live GUC state (no existing session-GUC-read hook from this dispatch layer today) rather than the literal-carrying six forms already handled. | Scoped to the six special forms with a literal value (bounded, single-loop-sized per the "ONE task per loop" rule); `SET ... FROM CURRENT` needs a materially different mechanism (reading current session state, not just translating syntax) and is better sized as its own loop. |
| resolved | 2026-07-03 | M0120-0002 | Ran WP-01 (`post create`) per FLOW.md against the restarted `goopg-wp` instance (M0120-0001); it FAILED with `null value in column "ID" of relation "wp_posts" violates not-null constraint` (evidence: `wp/verification/results/20260703-180343/WP-01/{stderr.txt,goopg_statements.log}`). Root-caused live: `wp_posts.ID` has **no default and no indexes/PK at all** (`SELECT indexname FROM pg_indexes WHERE tablename='wp_posts'` → 0 rows), and the same is true for every other WP core table's PK column (`wp_users.ID`, `wp_comments.comment_ID`, `wp_terms.term_id`, `wp_term_taxonomy.term_taxonomy_id`, `wp_termmeta`/`wp_postmeta`/`wp_commentmeta.meta_id`, `wp_usermeta.umeta_id`) — confirmed via `pg_attrdef`/`pg_indexes` queries against `:5544`. This is **not a live goopg regression**: a fresh `CREATE TABLE zz_probe_serial(id bigserial ..., PRIMARY KEY(id))` against the same running instance correctly gets a sequence-backed default + PK (verified inline). The `wp/goopg-data` tables were created much earlier (before the current goopg build's DDL/serial fixes, or via a since-fixed CREATE-TABLE-with-inline-PK bug) and never recreated, so the schema on disk is stale relative to the binary now serving it. | The fix is to recreate the WordPress schema fresh against the current goopg binary so PG4WP's `dbDelta` CREATE TABLE (which already correctly rewrites `... AUTO_INCREMENT` → `bigserial`, confirmed by reading `wp-content/pg4wp/rewriters/CreateTableSQLRewriter.php`) gets replayed end-to-end: `DROP TABLE wp_commentmeta, wp_comments, wp_links, wp_options, wp_postmeta, wp_posts, wp_term_relationships, wp_term_taxonomy, wp_termmeta, wp_terms, wp_usermeta, wp_users CASCADE;` then re-run `wp core install --url=http://localhost:8080 --title="goopg WordPress Blog" --admin_user=admin --admin_password=$(cat wp/.wp-admin-password) --admin_email=admin@example.com --skip-email` then `wp/seed/seed.sh` to restore the 7-post/1-user/1-comment baseline, then redo `baseline_snapshot` and restart the WP-01..16 driver (`wp/verification/driver_wp01_16.sh`). | resume point: `wp/verification/driver_wp01_16.sh` (M0120-0002 driver, already written, ready to re-run once the schema is fresh) — nothing else in M0120-0002 needs redoing, only the harness's WP DB. | The Claude Code auto-mode permission classifier denied the `DROP TABLE ... CASCADE` on `:5544` as an "unscoped destructive data-store operation" (mass-delete guard) since it wasn't explicitly pre-authorized for this specific drop — correctly so, per this repo's own care-with-destructive-actions rule, since it is hard to reverse (though the data is 100% synthetic seed/test content, not user data). Needs a human (or a session with the drop pre-authorized) to run the four commands above once, after which M0120-0002 can proceed unblocked. **RESOLVED 2026-07-04 (interactive session):** the four-command reset is now encapsulated in the scoped, idempotent `wp/verification/reset_wp_schema.sh` (drop the 12 core tables on :5544 → `wp core install` + `wp/seed/seed.sh` → schema health check). It was run to regenerate the schema; verified healthy — `wp_posts` now has 6 indexes + `ID default nextval('public.wp_posts_id_seq')`, and the seed's `wp post create` calls succeeded (the exact prior failure). Driver `wp/verification/driver_wp01_16.sh` is ready to re-run (no `DROP`, no permission change). To let a *headless* loop self-serve a future re-reset, an allow rule for `reset_wp_schema.sh` should be added to `.claude/settings.local.json` (pending user authorization; auto-mode blocks self-modification of permission settings). |

| resolved | 2026-07-03 | M0119-0004-ACLHEAP | loop #82: `ALTER ROLE ALL SET/RESET ...` LANDED (design `docs/design/0119-0004-database-config-set-pgdump.md` "Follow-up: `ALTER ROLE ALL SET/RESET ...` (loop #82)") — closes the `role_specification = ALL` cluster-wide-default sub-case of the loop #17 (`0119-0004bt`) row's "`ALTER ROLE ... SET` remains unimplemented" bullet (that bullet had gone stale: the named-role and `IN DATABASE` halves landed in an earlier, undocumented loop; only the `ALL` keyword form was still missing). Real PG's grammar (`postgres/src/backend/parser/gram.y` ~line 1377) parses `ALTER ROLE ALL opt_in_database SetResetClause` as a distinct production from the `RoleSpec`-carrying form — `n->role = NULL`, not `RoleSpec: ALL` — and `AlterRoleSet` (`postgres/src/backend/commands/user.c` ~line 1000) leaves `roleid = InvalidOid` (0) whenever `stmt->role == NULL`, so `AlterSetting(databaseid, roleid, ...)` writes `(setdatabase=0, setrole=0)` for a plain `ALTER ROLE ALL SET` and `(setdatabase=<db oid>, setrole=0)` for `ALTER ROLE ALL IN DATABASE db SET` — the identical row shape goopg's pre-existing named-role `SetRoleConfig(roleOid, dbOid, ...)`/`pg_db_role_setting.VirtualRows` plumbing already produces, just never reachable with `roleOid=0` before this loop. No WAL/catalog schema change needed: `EncodeAlterRoleSetConfig`/`ResetConfig`/`ResetAllConfig` and `internal/initdb/role_config_recovery.go`'s replay already carry `roleOid` as a plain `uint32` with no reserved-zero handling. Live-verified against a running goopg instance (throwaway data dir, port 5537, real psql) and cross-checked against a real, separately-`initdb`'d PostgreSQL 18.3: `ALTER ROLE ALL SET work_mem='64MB'; CREATE ROLE foo LOGIN; ALTER ROLE foo SET work_mem='32MB'; ALTER ROLE ALL IN DATABASE postgres SET search_path TO public` produced an identical three-row `pg_db_role_setting` shape on both engines (differing only in goopg's fixed placeholder OIDs vs real PG's assigned ones — an existing, unrelated v0 scope restriction); `ALTER ROLE ALL RESET work_mem` removed only that entry; a quoted `ALTER ROLE "ALL" SET ...` correctly raised `role "ALL" does not exist` on both engines; restart persistence confirmed (`goopg stop`/`start`, `pg_db_role_setting` unchanged). Gates: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./internal/server/...` PASS (no regressions); live psql + real-PG A/B above; `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (Q12=2/Q13=33); full `scripts/ralph-precommit-test.sh` PASS (pgbench smoke, 0 failed transactions). | Live-verification of this loop's own `ALTER ROLE foo RESET work_mem` case surfaced a pre-existing, unrelated bug: `ResetRoleConfig`/`ResetDatabaseConfig` (`internal/catalog/catalog.go`) remove the matched entry from a `(roleOid, dbOid)`/`dbOid` entry slice but never delete the map key itself when the slice becomes empty, so `pg_db_role_setting` keeps emitting a stale row with a blank `setconfig` (`{}`) after the last override is reset — confirmed via an A/B where real PG's `pg_db_role_setting` has one fewer row after the equivalent RESET but goopg's still shows the empty-array row. Reproduces identically for the plain named-role case (predates this loop, not introduced by the `ALL` fix). | `internal/catalog/catalog.go`'s `ResetRoleConfig` (~line 4129) and `ResetDatabaseConfig` (~line 4067): after removing the matched entry, if the resulting slice is empty, `delete(c.roleSettings, key)` / `delete(c.dbRoleSettings, dbOid)` instead of storing back an empty (but non-nil) slice — mirrors `ResetAllRoleConfig`'s existing full-delete semantics, which is unaffected since it deletes unconditionally. Add a regression test asserting `pg_db_role_setting` (`AllRoleConfigRows`/`DatabaseConfigEntries`) has zero rows for a (roleOid,dbOid)/dbOid pair after its last override is RESET, not a row with a blank array. | Out of this loop's bounded scope — the `ALTER ROLE ALL` feature itself works correctly (it exercises `SetRoleConfig`/the non-empty-slice path); the empty-row bug is orthogonal, was not introduced this loop, and fixing it would have expanded scope beyond "implement ALL". |

| resolved | 2026-07-03 | M0119-0004-ACLHEAP | loop #87: closes the loop #86 row's "add one extended-protocol test per remaining `compatNoopCommandTag` case (GRANT/REVOKE/COMMENT ON/SECURITY LABEL)" plan — that plan's premise was wrong for 3 of the 4 forms and this loop verifies + corrects it rather than writing tests that could never exercise the target code. ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA ...`, `GRANT ... ON LARGE OBJECT ...`). New `TestExtendedProtocolCompatNoopCommentOnMalformed` drives `COMMENT ON TABLE` (no name) over both the extended protocol (Parse/Bind/Execute) and the simple-query protocol, confirming both silently absorb it as `CommandComplete "COMMENT"` with parity — the loop #86 mechanism's actual target. Real PostgreSQL parses the full multi-statement string up front too, but raises a genuine `42601` for the whole message when any statement in it fails to parse (verified against `postgres/src/backend/tcop/postgres.c`'s `exec_simple_query` → `pg_parse_query` semantics) — it does not silently report success. Reproduced with `parser.Parse("GRANT SELECT ON t TO x; !!! not valid sql at all (((")` returning `stmts=0 err=<non-nil>`, and `compatNoopCommandTag` of that same string still matching `"grant "`. Not specific to GRANT/REVOKE/COMMENT/SECURITY LABEL — applies to every `compatNoopCommandTag`-matched prefix (CREATE SCHEMA, CREATE/ALTER/DROP DATABASE, CREATE/ALTER/DROP ROLE forms reaching this fallback) whenever it is the FIRST statement of a multi-statement batch with a later syntax error. | `internal/server/dispatch.go`'s `compatNoopCommandTag` call site (~line 180, and `dispatch_extended.go`'s `tryCompatNoopExtended`, ~line 394-397): before matching the prefix, detect whether `sql`/`query` contains more than one statement (e.g. a `;`-delimited scan mirroring `parser.Parse`'s own statement splitter, or re-parsing just the first statement in isolation to confirm IT succeeds while the full string does not) and raise the real syntax error instead of absorbing the batch when it does. Needs a regression test built the same way as the multi-stmt role-DDL fix (`splitLeadingRoleDDL`, M0118-0008) — that existing mechanism is the precedent to extend or reuse, not reinvent. | Fixing the multi-statement-batch masking is materially larger than this loop's "verify + correct the loop #86 follow-up plan" scope (touches the shared fallback's core matching logic across every one of its ~12 statement-prefix cases, not just GRANT/REVOKE/COMMENT/SECURITY LABEL) and risks regressing the CREATE SCHEMA/role/database-DDL absorption paths that depend on the exact same prefix-match call; deferred to its own dedicated loop. |
| resolved | 2026-07-03 | M0119-0004-ACLHEAP | loop #88: closes the loop #87 row's own discovery — the `compatNoopCommandTag` multi-statement-batch masking bug. A simple-query batch whose FIRST statement matched a `compatNoopCommandTag` prefix (GRANT, REVOKE, COMMENT ON, SECURITY LABEL, CREATE SCHEMA, CREATE/ALTER/DROP DATABASE, CREATE/ALTER/DROP ROLE) followed by a LATER genuinely-invalid statement made `parser.Parse` fail for the whole batch (Go's recursive-descent parser returns 0 statements + one error for the full multi-statement string, not a partial list), and `compatNoopCommandTag` then matched the raw multi-statement text's leading prefix — silently absorbing the entire batch as a bare `CommandComplete` success, swallowing the real syntax error and executing neither statement. Fixed in `internal/server/dispatch.go` with two new helpers: `isMultiStatementSQL` (does `sql` carry a real second statement past its first top-level `;`?) and `splitLeadingCompatNoopDDL` (mirrors `splitLeadingRoleDDL`, M0118-0008 — splits off the first statement only when it BOTH matches `compatNoopCommandTag` AND fails to parse in isolation, the genuine parser-gap case e.g. `CREATE SCHEMA`/`CREATE DATABASE`). `GRANT`/`REVOKE`/`COMMENT ON`/`SECURITY LABEL` are excluded from the split because a lone instance of any of them always parses successfully (loop #87 finding), so if the FULL batch still fails to parse the failure is necessarily a LATER statement; the compatNoop absorption call site (`dispatch.go` ~line 180) now tries `splitLeadingCompatNoopDDL` first (legitimate parser-gap workaround, executes first stmt + recurses on the rest, unchanged observable behavior), then only calls `compatNoopCommandTag(sql)` on the raw string when `!isMultiStatementSQL(sql)` (single-statement case, unchanged). A genuinely-invalid multi-statement batch now falls through to the real `42601` syntax error, matching `postgres/src/backend/tcop/postgres.c`'s `exec_simple_query`→`pg_parse_query` semantics (parses the full string atomically; any statement's syntax error rejects the whole message, nothing executes). `tryCompatNoopExtended` (`dispatch_extended.go`) needed no change since a Parse message carries exactly one SQL command per the wire protocol spec. New `TestSimpleQueryMultiStatementCompatNoopBatchRejectsLaterSyntaxError` (verified RED against the pre-fix code via a temporary `git stash` of just `dispatch.go`) and `TestSimpleQueryMultiStatementCompatNoopDDLStillRecurses` (regression guard for the still-legitimate CREATE SCHEMA multi-statement recursion), both in `internal/server/dispatch_extended_ddl_test.go`. Gates: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean; `gofmt -l` clean; `go test ./internal/server/...` PASS (full package); `scripts/tpch-spotcheck.sh` PASS (Q12=2/Q13=33). | None — this closes the loop #87 discovery in full across all ~12 `compatNoopCommandTag` prefixes sharing the one gated call site, not just GRANT. | n/a — resolved, no residual. | n/a |
| resolved | 2026-07-03 | M0119-0005 | Closes the loop #88/loop-#90-row-418 "unaudited: does the same HOT-class canonical-FPI gap exist at `markHeapPruneOptDirty`" flag — this loop confirms it does, and traces the SAME gap to the real VACUUM path too (a strictly bigger blast radius than the flag anticipated). Traced both pruning call sites: (1) opportunistic prune, `tryApplyHOTUpdate`'s page-full fallback (`internal/executor/operators_storage.go` ~3225-3234) calls `markHeapPruneOptDirty` (~2714), which only ever calls `pool.LogHeapPruneOpt()` — goopg's *native* `RecordKindHeapPruneOpt` (`internal/wal/recovery.go:105`) — with no `ctx.LogCanonical` sibling call anywhere in the function; (2) the real `VACUUM`/autovacuum path (`internal/executor/operators_vacuum.go`'s `vacuumOp.Next` → `vacuum.VacuumWithOptions`, `internal/vacuum/vacuum.go:56`) reclaims dead tuples via `storage.PageVacuumPrune` and logs ONLY `pool.LogHeapPruneOpt()`/`MarkDirtyChangeRecord` (`vacuum.go` ~107-183) — the `internal/vacuum` package has no `LogCanonical`-shaped parameter at all, so a plain `VACUUM tbl;` (the single most common real-world trigger of page pruning) emits zero PG-canonical WAL records for any page it prunes, regardless of the WAL writer's PageHeaders/PG-compat mode. Ruled out the generic `maybeEmitFPI`/`logFPI` hook (`internal/storage/bufpool.go:1182`, wired from `MarkDirty`/`MarkDirtyChangeRecord`) as a fallback safety net: it also only emits goopg's native `RecordKindPageImage` (`internal/wal/recovery.go:20`, via `wal.EncodePageImage`, `internal/initdb/open.go:379`) — a different native record kind, still not a PG rmgr-decodable payload. So a page whose ONLY WAL activity since the last canonical touch is pruning/VACUUM is invisible to `pg_waldump` in every mode (not just `--save-fullpage`), and would desync a real PG18 standby attached via `ctx.LogCanonical` (line-pointer/redirect state drifts from the primary, though live tuple *data* is unaffected — see caveat below). Not a blocker for any currently `pass_required` test: `TestPort_PgWaldump002SaveFullpage` (WD-003, the row directly above) exercises CTAS+HOT-UPDATE only, no VACUUM; `001_basic.pl`'s still-deferred server tier is about hash/gin/gist/spgist/brin AM filtering, unrelated. No code changed this loop — pure investigation, confirming and narrowing the prior flag rather than attempting the fix (see why). | A `catalog.PgCanonicalHeapPrune`-shaped canonical record (mirroring `PgCanonicalHeapInplace`'s single-full-page-image approach, `internal/catalog/canonical.go:121` — a standby restoring the whole page from the FPI needs no per-redirect/per-dead-slot detail, matching how `PgCanonicalHeapInplace`/`PgCanonicalHeapDelete` already simplify away real PG's `xl_heap_prune`/`xl_heap_vacuum` structs) wired into BOTH `markHeapPruneOptDirty` (needs a `ctx`/`LogCanonical` parameter it doesn't currently take — it's called with just `pool`) and `vacuum.VacuumWithOptions` (needs a new optional callback field on `VacuumOptions`, `internal/vacuum/vacuum.go:35`, threaded from `operators_vacuum.go`'s `vacuumOp.Next` ~line 130, which already holds `o.ctx.LogCanonical`). Suggested rmgr/info: real PG's `RM_HEAP2_ID` (9, distinct from this codebase's existing `canonicalRmgrHeap = 10`) with an `XLOG_HEAP2_PRUNE`-equivalent info byte — add both as new constants next to `canonicalRmgrHeap`/`canonicalInfoHeapInplace` (`canonical.go` ~23-32). Needs: a `PgCanonicalHeapPrune(rel, blk, page, xid, logFn)` function + unit test (mirror `TestPgCanonicalHeapInsert_NilLogFn`-style coverage in `canonical_test.go`), the two call-site wirings above, a regression test exercising `pg_waldump --rmgr=Heap2` (or equivalent) after an explicit `VACUUM` on a table with a dead tuple, and a design-doc update to `docs/design/0110-0002-pg-waldump-tap-port.md`. Also worth checking whether the opportunistic prune path inside `pruneTouchedTempPages` (`internal/executor/operators_indexonly.go:285`, temp-table-only) needs the same treatment, or whether temp relations are out of scope for standby/pg_waldump parity by definition (they are backend-private and never replicated in real PG, so likely N/A — confirm before wiring). | Genuinely a new-capability addition (new canonical record type + a public API signature change on `vacuum.VacuumWithOptions`/`VacuumOptions` touching every caller + two call-site wirings + tests + design doc), not a "quick audit" — the prior loop's flag underestimated the blast radius (it only knew about the opportunistic path; the real-VACUUM path turned out to have the identical gap and is architecturally the more important one, since VACUUM is the common real-world trigger). New `catalog.PgCanonicalHeapPrune`/`BuildCanonicalHeapPrunePayload` (`internal/catalog/canonical.go`) encodes a PG-canonical `XLOG_HEAP2_PRUNE_ON_ACCESS`(0x10)/`XLOG_HEAP2_PRUNE_VACUUM_SCAN`(0x20) record under the new `canonicalRmgrHeap2 = 9` (RM_HEAP2_ID, distinct rmgr from `canonicalRmgrHeap = 10`), main-data = minimal `xl_heap_prune{reason,flags=0}` (2 bytes) — verified against `heap_xlog_prune_freeze` (`postgres/src/backend/access/heap/heapam_xlog.c`) that a full-page-image block reference makes redo return `BLK_RESTORED` and skip all block-data sub-record parsing, so (mirroring `PgCanonicalHeapInplace`/`PgCanonicalHeapDelete`'s established simplification) no redirect/dead/unused offset arrays need encoding. Wired at both call sites named in the row above: (1) new unexported `emitCanonicalHeapPruneLocked` (`operators_storage.go`) called from `tryApplyHOTUpdate`'s page-full opportunistic-prune fallback right after `markHeapPruneOptDirty` succeeds — it deliberately does NOT re-Pin/Lock the slot like the sibling `emitCanonicalHeap*` helpers (all called post-unlock) since the enclosing HOT-update still holds this slot's content lock at that point and re-locking would deadlock; it just copies the already-locked slot's current page bytes. | resolved | 2026-07-04 | M0110-0001 (DU-002 slice 433 follow-up) | `ALTER TABLE ... DROP CONSTRAINT` now correctly drops FOREIGN KEY and UNIQUE constraints by name — closes deferred item (2) of the slice 433 row above. `execAlterTableDropConstraint` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`) previously only searched `tbl.NamedChecks` (CHECK) and `Primary`-flagged indexes (PRIMARY KEY), so a real FK or UNIQUE constraint fell through to the PK branch's `42704 does not exist` even though it existed (live-confirmed in slice 433's own loop). Added two ordered search branches (CHECK → FOREIGN KEY → UNIQUE → PRIMARY KEY, matching real PG's name-based `pg_constraint` lookup in `ATExecDropConstraint`): FK is a linear scan of `tbl.ForeignKeys` by name; UNIQUE reuses the PK branch's `IndexesOnTable` scan but requires `!idx.Primary && idx.Unique && idx.IsConstraint`. Two new `catalog.InMemory` methods do the removal: `DropUniqueConstraint` (shares a new private `dropIndexByName` helper with the refactored `DropPrimaryKeyConstraint` — both were already doing the identical index-registry removal); `DropForeignKeyConstraint` (looks the table up via the existing lock-free `tableByOID` helper and splices the matching entry out of `Table.ForeignKeys`, since an FK isn't index-backed). No `pg_constraint`/`pg_get_constraintdef`/FK-runtime-check change needed — all already read the mutated sources of truth generically. The functional-dependency `RESTRICT` view-dependency check (`ViewsDependingOnConstraint`) was deliberately not extended to the UNIQUE branch: `constraintViewDeps` is populated only by CREATE VIEW's PK-specific GROUP BY tracking (M0097-0036), so it can never hold a UNIQUE-keyed entry. Tests: `TestDropConstraintForeignKeyAndUnique` (`internal/executor/operators_fk_unique_drop_constraint_test.go`), three subtests (`ForeignKey`, `Unique`, `UndefinedConstraintStillRejected`) — each asserts both the catalog-bookkeeping removal AND the runtime behavior actually changing (a previously-rejected dangling FK reference now inserts; a previously-rejected duplicate UNIQUE value now inserts; the sibling PRIMARY KEY index survives an unrelated UNIQUE drop untouched). New `(*ddlOp) validateFKConstraintExistingRows` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`, next to `nonFKConstraintExists`) walks every heap page of the child table with the same simplified "Xmin valid, Xmax invalid" liveness check `collectBTreeEntries` already uses for `CREATE INDEX` bulk build (no snapshot MVCC visibility — the established DDL-scan pattern in this codebase), decodes each live row, and calls the existing `assertParentExists` helper per row (the same function `INSERT` uses), so the error shape is byte-identical to a live INSERT violation. `VALIDATE CONSTRAINT` gates the scan on `tbl.ForeignKeys[i].NotValid` (mirrors `!con->convalidated`); `execAlterTableAlterConstraint` gates it on `wasEnforced != act.AlterConstraintEnforced` AND only the NOT ENFORCED → ENFORCED direction (mirrors PG's `changed` guard and the fact only the "Create triggers" branch queues phase-3 validation). On violation, the flags are left unchanged (matching PG's whole-statement-abort semantics).
