Original row follows unedited for history. `ALTER VIEW` grammar completed in full — closes all three sub-forms slice 440 left open: `RENAME [COLUMN] old TO new` (was a silent no-op, same bug class as slice 440: the `rename`-token short-circuit already consumed it before falling to the catch-all), `ALTER [COLUMN] col SET DEFAULT expr / DROP DEFAULT` (PG's updatable-view column-default mechanism), and `SET`/`RESET (view_option_name [= value], ...)` covering all three PG `view_option_name`s per `postgres/doc/src/sgml/ref/alter_view.sgml` — the two booleans `security_barrier`/`security_invoker`, plus the enum `check_option` (`local`/`cascaded`, case-insensitive, PG's own `22023 invalid value for enum option` wording from `reloptions.c:1677`). All three reuse existing `AlterTableRenameColumn`/`AlterTableSetDefault`/`AlterTableDropDefault`/`AlterTableSetReloptions`/`AlterTableResetReloptions` actions and `execAlterTable`'s existing generic (relkind-agnostic) dispatch — no new executor action kinds needed. SET STORAGE`, no RENAME COLUMN or view involved): a multi-statement simple-query batch (`psql -c "stmt1; stmt2; stmt3"`, `\;`-joined) where an EARLIER statement calls the delete-then-resync catalog-heap pattern and a LATER statement in the same batch fails leaves the relation's `pg_attribute` heap rows completely empty (`pg_dump` emits `CREATE TABLE t (\n);` with zero columns) rather than rolling back the WHOLE implicit batch transaction the way real PostgreSQL's documented simple-query semantics require (a later failure in a multi-statement message should undo every earlier statement in that same message, including the `CREATE TABLE` — goopg instead partially rolls back: the table registration survives, but its catalog-heap sync does not). An atomicity violation, not merely a missing feature. | `internal/server/dispatch.go`'s `dispatchSimpleQueryViaExecutor` (~line 97) and its per-statement loop/implicit-transaction handling, cross-referenced against `deleteCatalogRowsForOID`/`syncTableToCatalogHeap` (`internal/executor/operators_ddl.go`) — likely an MVCC-visibility ordering issue where the resync's xmax/xmin stamps are decided independently of whether the enclosing implicit batch transaction ultimately commits. Minimal repro: `CREATE TABLE t (a int); ALTER TABLE t ALTER COLUMN a SET STORAGE external; SELECT 1/0;` as ONE `psql -c` batch (separate `-c` invocations do not reproduce it). | out of scope for this bounded parser-grammar loop: this is a WAL/MVCC-class transaction-atomicity bug (race detector + recovery/replication practice-card gates), spans the simple-query dispatch layer + the generic catalog-heap-resync mechanism used by many unrelated ALTER TABLE arms, and reproduces without any of this slice's diff. |

| resolved | 2026-07-04 | M0110-0001 (DU-002 slice 440 resume point (3)) | `ALTER SCHEMA name RENAME TO newname` / `ALTER SCHEMA name OWNER TO role` — the last open resume point of the slice-440 ledger row. goopg had no `RenameSchema`/schema-owner mechanism at all; added `catalog.InMemory.schemaOwners` (name→ownerOID map, default bootstrap superuser 10, wired into `pg_namespace.nspowner` which was previously hardcoded `"10"`) and `RenameSchema(old, new) ([]*Table, error)`, which re-keys the `schemas`/`schemaOwners` registry entries AND cascades into every `c.tables`/`c.indexes` entry whose `Schema` field names the old schema (goopg keys those catalogs by schema NAME, not OID like real PostgreSQL's `relnamespace`, per `postgres/src/backend/commands/schemacmds.c`'s `RenameSchema`/`AlterSchemaOwner`), re-pointing `Table.Schema`/`Index.Schema` and the map key together. Added dedicated `AlterSchemaStmt` parser node (previously "schema" fell into the blanket schema/view/collation/... compat-stub loop in `parseAlter`, a silent no-op like the pre-slice-440 ALTER VIEW gap) + `planner.go`/`dispatch.go` wiring (`ddlTag` → `"ALTER SCHEMA"`) + new WAL record kinds 100/101 (`RecordKindAlterSchemaRename`/`Owner`, `internal/wal/schema_alter_ddl.go`) with recovery replay in `internal/initdb/schema_ddl_recovery.go` (mirrors CREATE/DROP SCHEMA kinds 34/35) and a physical-replay no-op case in `wal.ApplyRecord` (mirrors the statistics-kind fix from the previous loop). Live-verified end-to-end (rename → cascaded table/sequence access under new name → owner change → pg_namespace round-trip → restart-persistence of name+owner+data+sequence-value) against goopg/psql. | resolved | 2026-07-04 | M0110-0001 (root-0024 follow-up, found while scoping residual (1)) | `internal/server/conn_tx.go`'s `connTxState.Session()` now gates on `c.active`, returning nil unless an explicit transaction is currently active, matching its own doc comment. Before this fix it unconditionally returned `c.sess`, which `Begin()` allocates lazily and `End()` never resets to nil (only `c.active` flips false) — so once a connection had run even one explicit transaction, `Session()` kept returning that same stale object forever after. dispatch.go's per-message wiring (`if sess := connTx.Session(); sess != nil { ectx.Session = sess } else { ectx.Session = <fresh throwaway> }`, the root-0024 original mechanism) then handed every LATER autocommit statement on that connection the reused session instead of a fresh message-scoped one; a successful autocommit `CREATE TABLE`'s `RecordDDLCreate` entry sat undrained in that reused session's `pendingDDL` (nothing drains it on success), until a wholly unrelated LATER aborting autocommit batch on the same connection ran `ProcessRollbackUndos` against that same reused session and incorrectly dropped the earlier, already-committed table too. Reproduced directly (`BEGIN;CREATE TABLE warm;COMMIT;` then a successful standalone `CREATE TABLE survivor` then an unrelated aborting `CREATE TABLE unrelated;SELECT * FROM missing;` — `survivor` was dropped). Test: `TestConnTxSessionNilWhenNotExplicit` (`internal/server/conn_tx_session_reuse_test.go`), confirmed RED on the pre-fix tree. | resolved | 2026-07-04 | M0110-0001 (root-0024 residual (1), mid-batch-BEGIN sub-part, loop #105) | Confirmed the mid-batch-`BEGIN` enum/composite residual flagged by the previous loop's row (`M0110-0001 (root-0024 residual (1), enum/composite half, loop #104)`) was already resolved as a side effect of that same loop's fix — no code change needed. That row's reasoning ("the `connTx.InExplicit()` write-back guard does NOT carry `ectx.Pending*` into `connTx` before `BEGIN` promotes the session") was traced and found incorrect: the write-back runs after every successful statement including `BEGIN` itself, and `connTx.InExplicit()` is already `true` by the time `BEGIN`'s own statement completes (`connTx.Begin()` runs earlier in the same `TxBegin` case) — so the write-back right after `BEGIN` already carries any pre-`BEGIN` autocommit `ectx.PendingCreatedEnums`/etc. into `connTx`. Verified empirically with throwaway probes before landing four permanent regression tests in `internal/server/dispatch_batch_atomicity_test.go`: `TestSimpleQueryMidBatchBeginUndoesEarlierAutocommitCreateType` (covers both the plain-`ROLLBACK`-same-message form and the real-PG-accurate form where a mid-batch error aborts the whole message and the actual `ROLLBACK` arrives in a separate follow-up message), `TestSimpleQueryMidBatchBeginUndoesEarlierAutocommitCreateComposite`, `TestSimpleQueryMidBatchBeginUndoesEarlierAutocommitAddValue`. Design doc `docs/design/root-0024-simple-query-batch-ddl-create-atomicity.md` new "Follow-up: mid-batch-`BEGIN` combination confirmed already resolved for enum/composite" section; `docs/design/README.md`'s root-0024 row updated in the same commit. | Nothing new deferred by this loop — this closes root-0024's first residual for enum/composite entirely (both the single-message case from loop #104 and this mid-batch-`BEGIN` case). | N/A — fully closed for enum/composite.
